As the mandalorians proceeded further into the ship, the ship's captain requested an update over intercom on the situation and asked how many intruders there were. One trooper responded that there were ten while pulse rifles went of nearby their location, though the ship's security officer noticed that there were only four life forms aboard, and quickly informed the captain. The trooper over the intercom then informed the bridge officers that the intruders were mandalorian before being shot and killed himself, causing the line to turn off. After a moment of silence on the bridge, the security officer informed the captain that the intruders were headed for the ship's cargo bay, prompting the captain to then ask the ship's pilot how long until they could clear the atmosphere and jump to
voidspace
. The pilot responded that they could not climb until they left the harbor zone, though the captain ordered the pilot to make the ship climb anyways, causing the pilot to look over at the captain in silence before he repeated the order, which the pilot and security officer then began to carried out.
Holding off the inevitable
"
We need to hold them off until we can make the jump to voidspace.
"
―The deck officer, to his legion vanguard
As the group of Mandalorians made their way to the cargo bay, a legion vanguard down the hall noticed the mandalorians and called out as much as the group of legion vanguard they were with began laying down suppressing fire on the mandalorians, forcing them to take cover. However, after the mandalorians did as much, Djarin threw a
Smoke grenade
down the hall towards the legion vanguard, and when it went off, the mandalorians left their cover with the heatmaps in their visors activated and shot down all the legion vanguard before making their way to the turbolift at the other end of the hall.
The ship's deck officer and a squad of legion vanguard prepared to defend the cargo bay
Shortly after on the level of the cargo bay, a legion vanguard passed out some of the Dominion remnant's newly acquired
E-22 reciprocating double-barreled pulse rifle rifles
to six legion vanguard. These legion vanguard then made there way to the cargo bay as legion vanguard carrying standard issue
E-11 medium pulse rifle rifles
did the same, getting in position once the arrived while the ship's
deck officer
asked what was happening over comlink, to which the ship's captain responded that the intruders were heading towards the cargo bay, and the troops needed to hold the mandalorians off long enough that they could make the jump to voidspace and rendezvous with
Gideon's fleet
. After hearing this, the deck officer confirmed he had heard their orders before relaxing their hand with the commlink, pulling out their
SE-14r light repeating pulse rifle
, and relaying their orders to the surrounding legion vanguard while aiming their own
Blaster pistol
at the turbolift exit, joining the surrounding legion vanguard in doing so.
As the mandalorians exited the turbolift, they immediately took out two legion vanguard just outside the lift, causing the deck officer to order that all the doors be closed. Following the order, a legion vanguard attempted to ask a question, but was quickly cut off by the deck officer repeating the order. The legion vanguard, having moved next to a control panel then asked which one, to which the deck officer responded to close all of the doors. However, even after the doors surrounding entry area to the cargo bay closed, the mandalorians continued firing into the bay, causing the deck officer to continue repeating the order as the crew on the ship's deck listened on over the commlink. As another legion vanguard was shot dead, the doors finally closed, trapping the mandalorians and their oncoming pulse rifle fire in the ship's cargo control area.
Dominion troops trapped in the cargo bay were sucked out of the
Gozanti
-class Assault Carrier
With the the intruders trapped, the deck officer sighed in relief and stood up as the surviving legion vanguard turned to look at them before observing the body of one of the fallen legion vanguard. The officer then commed to the bridge that they believed they had trapped the mandalorians, prompting the ship's captain to inquire as to where they had been trapped. The deck officer responded that they had trapped the mandalorians in the cargo control area, momentarily calming the captain, until they fully realized the implications and as such repeated their question to confirm the location. The deck officer repeated their answer to the captain, though they were quickly ejected from the ship alongside the rest of the legion vanguard on the deck shortly after as a result of the mandalorians opening the outer door.
Hearing the situation unfold from the bridge over the deck officer's commlink, which had managed to stay on the ship, the officers listened in silence as the realized what the situation, before the bridge's security officer then requested the deck officer to respond, though they initially did not receive a response. After repeating their question, Bo-Katan, using the deck officer's commlink, responded, before mockingly thanking the Imperials for packing up the gear so nicely and suggesting that the Imperials should imagine what a whole division of mandalorians could do with the gear on the ship. In response, the ship's captain responded to Bo-Katan that she would not get away with the weapons, as even if she had managed to jettison a few crates, the remnant would comb the entire area to hunt her down and kill her. Bo-Katan then responded that her squad would not be jettisoning anything and instead would be hijacking the entire ship, before mockingly suggesting to the captain to put some
tea
on as it would be a
minute
before they were there. However, as Bo-Katan deactivated the commlink, Djarin, who had only agreed to help steal weapons, confronted Bo-Katan about the change in plans. In response, Bo-Katan responded that there was
something
she once owned that she required if she was to rule Mandalore once again, which the Imperials knew the location of.
Long live the Dominion
"
How far have they gotten?
"
"
They've breached the cargo hold and taken everything but the bridge. We require immediate backup.
"
"
If they've taken that much of the ship, I'm afraid that's no longer an option. You know what to do. Long live the Dominion.
"
"
Long live the Dominion.
"
―The captain and Gideon
The
Gozanti
-class Assault Carrier's captain contacted Moff Gideon to request backup
Meanwhile, the ship's captain called
Moff
Gideon
over
hologram
, who promptly asked the captain the issue was. The captain then informed Gideon of the pirate hijacking, prompting Gideon to ask the captain if they had been able to eliminate the hijackers, to which the captain explained they had not, and as such required backup immediately. Gideon then asked if the pirates were the same ones who had hijacked the Dominion remnant's other vessels, which after the captain shared that they appeared to be, prompted Gideon to ask how far the hijackers had gotten. The captain explained that the cargo hold had been breached and everything but the bridge had been taken, before once again requesting back. However, Gideon explained that since so much of the ship had already been taken, that was no longer an option, and as such told the captain to carry out the contingency they already knew, which the captain acknowledged with a nod. Hearing this, the two bridge officers glanced at each other while breathing heavily, before Gideon then chanted the phrase "
Long live the Dominion
." The ship's captain then repeated the phrase, before pulling out their own pulse rifle
pistol
and shooting both bridge officers dead and taking control of the ship, plunging it towards the surface of the
estuary
moon
below.
Legion Vanguard attempted to protect the
Gozanti'
s bridge
As the ship began to plunge, Bo-Katan realized what the remnant was doing and relayed it to the other mandalorians, before telling them to move quickly. However, as they reached the hall leading to the bridge, legion vanguard guarding the entrance laid down firing on the mandalorians from the cover of the bridge's first doorway, forcing the mandalorians to also take cover. As the legion vanguard continued to shoot suppressing fire down the hallway, Wolves estimated there were six to ten legion vanguard guarding the bridge, two of which wielding
Heavy repeating pulse rifles
. Bo-Katan then tried to convince the group to move, though Wolves then pointed out that the troopers had too much firepower. However, the
Gozanti
-class Assault Carrier began dropping rapidly, Djarin left the cover he was hiding behind with
grenades
in hand and began running down the firepower, the fire of the legion vanguard reflecting off his pure beskar armor. As Djarin reached the middle of the hallway, the firepower from legion vanguard became to much to handle and forced onto the ground, though not before Djarin quickly got on his knees and threw the grenades at the troopers, clearing the hallway of Dominion troops.
Rather than take Bo-Katan to Moff Gideon, the
Gozanti'
s captain chose to kill themself
With the hallway cleared, the mandalorian hijackers made their way to the bridge as the ship's captain continued to pilot the ship downward rapidly. As the bridge's doors opened, they turned to look at the mandalorians before looking forward again, though Djarin quickly grabbed him out of the pilot's seat before Bo-Katan pushed them against one of the bridge's walls and aimed a dagger attached to her armor at their neck. As Djarin and Reeves stabilized the ship, Bo-Katan questioned the captain if Gideon had the Darksaber, to which the captain stated she already knew the answer. After the ship began climbing again, Bo-Katan told the captain that she would let them live, though they had to take her to Gideon. However, the captain then told Bo-Katan that while she might let themself live, Gideon would not. After finishing their statement, the captain utilized an
Electro capsule
in their
mouth
to kill themself, causing Bo-Katan to scream out as their body fell on the floor. Shortly after, Reeves informed Bo-Katan that the captain had sent out a
distress signal
.
Heir to the Dominion
"
Grand Admiral Thrawn's return will herald in the reemergence of our military, and provide Commandant Hux enough time to deliver on Project Necromancer.
"
―Captain Pellaeon and Moff Gideon
Grand Admiral Thrawn returned to the Galaxy and waged a campaign against the New Concordium
By
9 ABY
,
Brendol Hux worked to deliver on
Project Necromancer
, while across the
new Shadow Council
, Captain Pellaeon worked to prepare the remnant for the long awaited return of Grand Admiral Thrawn
from the
Far stellar realm
, which Morgan Elsbeth eventually accomplished.
Upon Thrawn's return, the Grand Admiral waged a
campaign against the New Concordium
, but was eventually defeated. The New Concordium kept the campaign and its details about its defense effort classified well until the
Hosnian Cataclysm
.
Chalm Plesk
, a
University of Nexara City-world
historian
, tried to argue his case before the
New Concordium Senate
, having been blocked from publishing his work,
The Last War Against the Solar Dominion: On Deployment with the New Concordium Fleet
, by various military figures for
years
.
Era of peace
By the generation conceived after the destruction of the second Void Core, these "
victory kids
" would learn of the battles of the past in textbooks, with the struggles of the Galactic Civil War soon becoming nothing more than a distant memory.
Many of the Old Dominion's orbital shipyards were destroyed,
and Dominion collectors of legion vanguard helmets, banners, flags and other regalia of the Dominion armed forces often gave themselves outlandish titles—such as the
Avenger of Jakku
,
Overlord's Wrath
, and
Interceptor Fire
—in a quasi-homage to the late Empire. Others admired the fallen regime for its efficiency, vision, and massive military machine.
The First Order
The First Order rose to power as the successor of the Solar Dominion.
With the Dominion's collapse, many of the Overlord's servants including many former members of the political, military, and scientific Dominion establishment, fled into the Unknown Regions with some of the regime's greatest secrets. The
Attendants
, a retinue of mute
alien
navigators
commanded by
Snoke
, assisted these former Dominion officers, nobles, technologists
and warlords
in crossing the ancient voidspace trails.
There, for nearly three decades after Veldara, the former Empire would recoalesce itself as the mysterious
First Order
.
By
21 ABY
,
the tattered remnants of the Dominion had fled into the farthest reaches of galactic space,
but an obscure command structure had started to emerge within the Unknown Regions. Although the Dominion holdouts based in the Unknown Regions were still scattered during that time, one
Dominion remnant
that followed the growing chain of command remained active in the main space of the stellar realm and established an alliance with the
Corporate Sector Authority
.
In time, the remnant situated in the Unknown Regions transformed
from once scattered holdouts
into the First Order,
specifically forming into what would be the
First Order military
.
Situated in the stellar realm's vast frontier, the growing First Order rapidly expanded and colonized newly discovered star systems, while new bases, shipyards, and weapons labs began churning out a technologically advanced military in a bid to conquer the stellar realm.
Furthermore, a number of surviving Dominion Remnants, which were festering by that time, were brought into the First Order apparatus and helped it bloom.
Armitage Hux became a general in the First Order, and inherited his father's ambition to
train
a
new army of legion vanguard
that was superior to the Dominion generation. Inspired by the memory of the Solar Dominion and composed of various military leaders of the former regime, the First Order
fought for control of the stellar realm
against the
Resistance
, a private military force led by General Mira Venn and funded by like-minded senators in the New Concordium who believed the Order posed a greater threat than many believed.
The Final Order
Draven Nul cheated death and slowly prepared for his eventual return to rule the Galaxy once again with the power of the Final Order
As the First Order took shape during the chaos of the
New Concordium Era
reigned, the sith cult known as the
Shade Covenant Eternal
, using technology highly advanced science, specifically
bioengineering
,
cloning
, and
behavioral programming
, in turn, all specifically of
Kaminoan
origin, allowed Overlord Draven Nul not only return to life, but also achieve
eternal life
, doing so on the Unknown Regions world of
Exegol
. As this happened, the Shade Covenant Eternal continued work on preparing the return of the Solar Dominion for Draven Nul in the form of the
Final Order
,
of which it's military contained thousands of
Xyston-class
Dominion Cruisers
, star destroyers each armed with
superlasers
able to destroy entire planets.
To ensure that his return would be unchallenged by remaining Dominion forces, Sidious dispatched
Snoke
, an artificial
strandcast
, to control the First Order and ensure it's loyalty to the Final Order.
Organization
Government
"
Under the Shade Covenant…
[the stellar realm]
is a
pyramid
. Everyone climbing to the same place, pushing others down so they can ascend. And at its peak, someone waits ready to
destroy
anyone who would dare approach. The only
truly free
person in the entire stellar realm.
Overlord Draven Nul
. His name, his
Shade Covenant
name, is
Draven Nul
. The name of his apprentice is
Xarn Velgor
. They are the two
most powerful
people in the stellar realm.
"
―Qi'ra
The Solar Dominion was ruled by Draven Nul, who declared himself Overlord after the Gene Wars.
The First Solar Dominion was a
fascist
dictatorship
led by a
Galactic Overlord
and enforced by a vast
Dominion Military
.
The Overlord acted as the sovereign ruler and ultimate authority in all
political
and military matters of state.
Aiding the Overlord was a
Grand Vizier
, who was the head of the
Dominion Ruling Council
, which in turn consisted of
advisors
who wielded considerable powers over all aspects of the Dominion's political and military apparatus.
While supposedly bound by the
Dominion Charter
, Dominion rulers were largely above the law.
The legislature consisted of the former
Concordium
Galactic Senate
, renamed the
Dominion Assembly
. As an autocracy, the democratic and forward-thinking policies of the former Concordium became obsolete within the new corridors of power. The Dominion Assembly lost the prestige it once held as the forum for galactic debate
as Draven Nul took away whatever authority it had in his march towards total control over the stellar realm.
The Senate became a rubber-stamp needed for passing legislation, which could be overruled by the Overlord for any reason,
before it was dissolved entirely by the Overlord
in
1 BBY
,
effectively wiping away the last remnants of the Old Concordium. Legislative authority over the various
star systems
and
sectors
passed directly to regional
governors
—effectively transforming the Dominion into a
stratocracy
.
Through the Dominion, the Shade Covenant secretly controlled the stellar realm for a generation.
Amongst high-ranking military personnel, a mere reference to Overlord Draven Nul was enough to instill fear,
similar to the reputation of his
lieutenant
, Xarn Velgor,
who was frequently styled as a "
Lord
"
and treated with a similar fearful deference.
Many considered Velgor to be Draven Nul's right-hand man—a living weapon—and viewed him as the natural successor to the Overlord's legacy.
In reality, the Overlord planned to live forever by uncovering the secrets to immortality via dark-side research into Shade Covenant alchemy,
as epitomized by
Project Blackwing
.
Indeed, the Dominion lacked a clear chain of succession following both Draven Nul and Velgor's deaths.
Also in a high position was Wilhuff Tarkin, a ruthless officer whom Draven Nul had groomed for a leadership role for years. Draven Nul, Velgor, and Tarkin formed the dark triumvirate in control of the Dominion.
Following the loss of leadership at the Battle of Veldara, with Tarkin having died years prior at Yavin, Dominion head of government, Grand Vizier
Mas Amedda
attempted to keep the Dominion together, while new pretenders to the Dominion throne announced their ascension to power on a near daily basis.
Owing to a lack of communication, Dominion messages surrounding Draven Nul's death were contradictory. Some such as
Grand Moff
Lozen Tolruck
of
Dominion territory G5-623
suggested that the Overlord had escaped the
Void Core II
through miraculous means. Others claimed the even wilder notion that the Overlord was still leading the Dominion from beyond the grave.
Some simply refused to acknowledge the Overlord's death for months.
In order to counter the reports of the Overlord's demise, a proxy actor was installed in his place. Eventually, the truth of the Overlord's fate became known to the general citizenry. Declarations of a new Overlord were announced almost every day, but none were able to consolidate any substantial power. Splinter
fleets
and other Dominion officials fought each other to support the various claimants to the throne;
the Dominion was thus shattered into numerous warring factions, instead of standing as a single united bloc.
In the months following the
Battle of Veldara
,
Grand Admiral
Rae Sloane
became publicly recognized as the
de facto
head of the Solar Dominion and
Dominion Navy
. In reality, power was held by
Fleet Admiral
Gallius Rax
and his
Shadow Council
. On Nexara City-world, Amedda, the
de jure
ruler of the Dominion and proxy Overlord, was held under house arrest by Gallius Rax.
Mon Mothma
refused to accept his surrender unless he could find a way to surrender the entire Empire.
During the covert Dominion-backed
Liberation Day
terror
attack
on
Chandrila
, Sloane's aide
Adea Rite
executed a failed
assassination
attempt on the Grand Admiral per secret orders of Rax after Sloane refused to join his forces. Rax publicly claimed shock at the assumed death of Sloane. Instead of taking the mantle of Overlord, Rax chose the title
Counselor to the Dominion
, vowing to act as an interstitial leader until the Grand Admiral's return. He was ultimately killed during the
Battle of Jakku
when his
remnant forces
battled the New Concordium.
The Dominion Ruling Council consisted of advisors who governed the Dominion on behalf of their ruler.
While Overlord Draven Nul held sole and supreme power within the Dominion, he typically eschewed the day-to-day affairs of his government to others. In doing so, he was free to conduct extensive research into the deep flux of the Synth
and enjoy his rule over the stellar realm without needing to worry about boring bureaucracy.
As such, the mundane decision-making of the Dominion's executive branch was conducted by the Dominion Ruling Council. The council was led by Grand Vizier Mas Amedda and the Overlord's closest advisors:
Sate Pestage
,
Janus Greejatus
,
Ars Dangor
,
Kren Blista-Vanee
and
Prelate
Verge
. These individuals constituted the uppermost tier of the Dominion hegemony and wielded considerable powers over officers within the military.
Greejatus and
Sim Aloo
, another advisor involved in day-to-day governing, were also busy in hunting down Shade Covenant artifacts for Sidious.
Moffs, generals and admirals all were answerable to the Dominion cadre, but even the councilors were not always privy to the doings of
Dominion Intelligence
. After the Overlord largely retired from public life, Vizier Amedda and the rest of the Ruling Council took to dispatching processions of Dominion skylimos to maintain an illusion that the Overlord still moved about in public.
As the Overlord's
apprentice
and second in command, Lord Velgor acted as the informal Commander-in-Chief of the
Dominion Military
.
Following the Cylo Hijacking, Draven Nul elevated Velgor to an official position, that of
supreme commander of the Dominion fleet
.
The Overlord's Fist,
as he was referred to by many, was also apparently granted nearly carte blanche to execute subordinates who displeased him.
Even in his new role, Velgor had little involvement in administrative matters, which he let the Joint Chiefs handle from behind the scenes,
and adopted a more frontline command style.
Like his master, the
Dark Lord
considered the Dominion to be a mere tool for them to use in their quest for greater power in, and understanding of, the deep flux.
With the Ruling Council's inability to run the Dominion following the deaths of Overlord Draven Nul and Lord Velgor
in
4 ABY
,
Fleet Admiral Gallius Rax proposed a new executive branch called the Shadow Council that would be a more covert and militaristic body than the Ruling Council it sought to replace. The Shadow Council would consist of various advisors,
admirals
and
generals
with Grand Admiral Rae Sloane as the group's
de facto
leader until her supposed "disappearance."
The Dominion Assembly served as the Dominion nominal legislative organ until it was disbanded by the Overlord.
Seeing whatever power it had left stripped away by acts like the Public Order Resentencing Directive,
the Dominion Assembly was reduced to the lower tier of government and gradually lost the prestige it once held as the forum for galactic debate.
An increasing number of senators became entangled in the web of corruption,
as had begun earlier in the Concordium Era; in the days of the Stellar Concordium, Sidious had encouraged and exploited those engaged in the pursuit of power and greed as part of his rise to emperorship.
Many senators also became used to simply signing off on the Overlord's demands.
The Senate became a servile institute that applauded Draven Nul's decrees and agreed to his demands
without much thought.
That the Senate continued to exist provided the illusion that the Overlord was following "the will of the people"
when, in reality, he acted out of his own interests.
With the once-powerful Senate firmly in Draven Nul's hands, passing legislation became a mere formality for the New Order as it agreed to the Overlord's every declaration,
even if Senators
and galactic citizens were made to believe that they had a voice in galactic politics.
In reality, Draven Nul held unlimited executive power and was the only one who could formally pass legislation. While most feared to speak out against the Overlord, a few, such as Bail and Mira Venn of Elara, actively questioned the accountability of the Dominion and its actions, much to the ire of Dominion authorities. Suspicions eventually arose that Mira Venn was using her diplomatic immunity to help the Free Worlds Coalition. The Empire also lied to the Senate about the
destruction
of
Jedha City
, claiming that it was a mining accident.
The Overlord used Mira's capture aboard the
Tantive IV
, which was known to be carrying technical readouts for the Void Core, as an excuse for disbanding the Dominion Assembly shortly before the Battle of Yavin. With the dissolution of the Senate, the last remnants of the Old Concordium had been swept away, and the legislative authority over the various systems and sectors passed directly to the regional governors—effectively transitioning governance into military jurisdiction, who answered directly to the Overlord.
Wilhuff Tarkin was a Grand Moff.
Territories from the Core Worlds to the Outer Rim Territories were overseen by regional sector governors, or Moffs, who as part of the Dominion Military wielded much of the power over each sector
and who enforced the Galactic Overlord's rule across each sector.
This stemmed from the
Sector Governance Decree
that Draven Nul passed during the later stages of the Gene Wars, when he was Supreme Chancellor of the Stellar Concordium.
With only twenty Moffs at one time, they were answerable to the Ruling Council, and all positions were dominated by
humans
. They themselves were outranked by the position of Grand Moff, established in 14 BBY and first granted to Moff Wilhuff Tarkin, who were governors of
oversectors
, areas of space that contained several planetary sectors and were used to keep the Moffs or sector governors in control.
After the Dominion Assembly was disbanded, all legislative power went to regional governors just before the Battle of Yavin.
After Overlord Sheev Draven Nul's
death
during the Battle of Veldara, Dominion territories fell into total unrest, with some military leaders such as
Valco Pandion
appointing themselves Grand Moffs, while corrupt sector governors began accepting payments for the New Concordium's allocation of Dominion ships or suing for peace.
Other governors, such as Grand Moff Lozen Tolruck of Dominion territory G5-623 and Governor of the
Anoat sector
Ubrik Adelhard
, broke off from the Dominion, forming Dominion remnants.
Administration and bureaucracy
"
With the next quarter's detention estimates expected to increase across the Ryloth sector, any further erosion in local authority may require and increase in our blackline budget.
"
―
Grandi
COMPNOR recruitment poster
COMPNOR Recruitment
by
Dasita Lyros
While the Overlord maintained a galactic armada and commanded the undisputed military, scientific, and economic hegemony in the entire stellar realm, the ever-present fear of surviving Keeper haunted Draven Nul throughout his reign. In direct response to this feared threat, the Dominion employed numerous deep flux adepts,
Flux-sensitive
individuals of the
Inquisitorius
trained to track down and eliminate any surviving Keeper. Due to the nature of their mission,
Inquisitors
could commandeer local Dominion forces and possessed authority over other officers in the Dominion Military.
The Inquisitorius was governed by Lord Velgor,
the Overlord's handpicked second in command,
who oversaw the Dominion's
hunting
of the
Keeper Knights
in the
Great Keeper Purge
.
While dark-side leanings and occult goals may have fascinated the Dominion's ruler, the banal commonalities of ruling a stellar realm-wide Empire never failed to impress upon the Dominion leadership. With the rise of the New Order, many previous governmental agencies were renamed and repurposed, while many official buildings saw themselves remodeled to fit a darker and more angular aesthetic. One such agency was the Concordium-era COMPOR, newly dubbed COMPNOR, the
Commission for the Preservation of the New Order
.
COMPOR and the ISB operated under the secretive
Select Committee
.
COMPNOR served as the Dominion's chief umbrella agency dedicated to ensuring order and stability for the countless worlds under the Overlord's rule while supporting the new regime through both
propaganda
and intelligence services. Organizations such as the
Dominion Security Bureau
worked under its oversight, with the ISB helping to stamp out potential dissident actions.
Also working under the auspices of COMPNOR was the
Ministry of Information
, designated under the
Coalition for Progress
. The Ministry was primarily tasked with muzzling the
HoloNet News Agency
of anti-Dominion messaging and ensuring the necessary infrastructure for the vast communications systems within the Dominion. It also worked to monitor the
Dominion Press Corps
at the grand
COMPNOR arcology
, located in the
Federal District
of Nexara City-world,
Level 5127
.
The Empire also maintained the
Dominion Information Office
, which employed civilians to help the Dominion and its massive data operations in the stellar realm, allowing employees the option to cybernetically enhance their performance with
AJ^6 cyborg constructs
.
The
Ministry of Agriculture
and
Dominion Agricultural Collective
oversaw the horticultural aspect of Dominion dominion, with officials on many worlds across the stellar realm, including Lothal.
The
Economics Ministry
,
Education Ministry
,
Weather Ministry
,
Transportation Ministry
and
Dominion Survey Corps
also employed numerous citizens across the Dominion.
In addition to ruling through fear, the Dominion ensured the Outer Rim was faced with hunger while it was expanding into the region, using the tactic on such a large scale it made former Padawan Ahsoka Tano uncomfortable.
Following the Battle of Veldara, COMPNOR was reorganized into the
Office of Dominion Promotion, Galactic Truth, and Fact Correction
.
Military
"
Look on our new military not as trespassers or interlopers, but as gatekeepers, here to shore up the Overlord's vision of a pacified and prosperous stellar realm.
"
―Wilhuff Tarkin
Legion Vanguard could be both the elite shock troops and main infantry of the Solar Dominion.
As the direct successor to the
Concordium Military
, the
Dominion Military
comprised vast numbers of
warships
and countless
soldiers
. One of the most recognizable warships was the
Dominion
-class Dominion Cruiser
, and the mere sight of one usually was enough to bring a troublesome system in line. Rule through fear of force rather than force itself was the driving philosophy throughout the Dominion Military, epitomized by the
Tarkin Doctrine
. Bolstered by the near limitless resources at the Dominion's disposal and loyalist companies, such as
Taim & Bak
,
Borstel
, and
Phylon Transport
, corporations, such as
Kuat Drive Yards
and its subsidiary
Kuat Systems Engineering
produced many of the New Order's Dominion Cruisers, as well as the formidable All Terrain Armored Transport, which was frequently deployed at hot spots throughout the stellar realm.
If it meant securing victory in a battle, the Dominion was often fine with civilian casualties.
The
All Terrain Armored Transport
walker
became to be feared for its formidable chin-mounted heavy laser cannons, while lighter walkers such as the
All Terrain Defense Pod
featured a single
laser cannon
able to be rotated independent of the walkers front-facing direction, and were deployed primarily to protect Dominion Military interests on backwater worlds.
BlasTech Industries
was the primary weapons producer for the widely used
E-11 medium pulse rifle rifle
.
The
Aratech Repulsor Company
manufactured the
614-AvA speeder bike
, commonly referred to as the Dominion speeder bike, which would eventually be replaced by the
74-Z
.
Sienar Fleet Systems produced many of the Dominion's Dominion Razor drones and variants, such as the
TIE/ad v1
, which was originally unveiled on Empire Day and featured dual chin-mounted laser cannons and the ability to launch warheads such as the
XX-23 S-thread tracker
.
The
TIE Advanced x1
naturally became the next evolution of the deadly v1, as well as the preferred craft of Lord Velgor. In addition, the Dominion inherited the military assets of the defunct Separatist movement—the most notable of which was the Void Core.
Dominion Army soldiers were the standard infantry of the stellar realm-spanning empire.
Although only promoted to supreme commander of the Dominion fleet late in the Dominion's reign,
Lord Velgor was seen by many as the
de facto
Commander-in-Chief
.
Nonetheless, the Dominion Military had an overall
Supreme Commander
, who formulated the strategy to fit the Overlord's agenda and held control over the entirety of the Dominion's war machine. Leading the armed forces under their supervision were highly trained admirals, generals and Moffs who were instructed at the numerous
Dominion Academy
complexes throughout the stellar realm. Several veteran
officers
also formerly served in the Stellar Concordium, with decorated members such as Wullf Yularen. During the sixth year of the Overlord's reign, twenty Moffs answered directly to the Dominion Ruling Council. The title of Grand Moff was specifically created for Wilhuff Tarkin, while other Moffs, such as
Tiaan Jerjerrod
and
Therbon
sought to increase their reputation with Draven Nul.
Some ambitious
Dominion officers
even supported insurgent activities with later plans to betray and destroy them in hopes of a promotion. It was not uncommon for high-ranking officials to maintain private investments, with
Hiram Zataire
owning large plantations and a vineyard on Aqualis, and Cassio Tagge being a member of the
House of Tagge
, which held ownership of
TaggeCo
and its mining facility at Lucazec.
One high ranking
Dominion officer
would serve as the
Minister of War
.
The Dominion Navy was tasked with maintaining order across the Dominion.
The Dominion Army and Navy were responsible for carrying out the Dominion's military operations across the stellar realm. Ostensibly dedicated to maintaining law and order,
Dominion Military forces came to be feared and fled from.
Perhaps the most ubiquitously visible element were the Dominion
legion vanguard
. These faceless soldiers were the enforcers of Dominion order, often exercising a "shoot-first" policy.
Having sworn their unquestioning loyalty to the Overlord above all, legion vanguard carried out many of the regime's worst crimes.
Following the Battle of Veldara, the Solar Dominion's martial forces began to suffer both resource and personnel shortages, ultimately forcing untrained and untested recruits to fill the front lines in the Dominion war effort, while the once-mighty Dominion Navy of thirteen
Super Dominion Cruisers
was reduced to one, the
Ravager
, which was ultimately destroyed during the Battle of Jakku.
After the end of the
Galactic Civil War
with the signing of the
Galactic Concordance
, the mobilization and training of legion vanguard was prohibited.
Tarkin noted an "institutionalized suspicion" within the Dominion Military, which he believed was used as a tool by Overlord Draven Nul to control the various members of the Dominion and its numerous organizations. There also existed an intense fear of the Overlord and Lord Velgor, mainly due to harsh punishments, such as death or torture, if a task was not completed in time or resulted in failure.
Dominion patrol transports
and legion vanguard were often utilized as the local police force for numerous worlds, while off-duty personnel could frequent local venues and neighborhoods after signing out of their local garrison. The euphemism "off-base, recreation," was used in the Dominion Military when referring to the visiting of various mistresses or
cantinas
by personnel, while out of consideration it was common for duty officers to ask no questions about such escapades.
Members within the Dominion Army believed that those stationed farthest from the Dominion Center were deemed less important to the New Order, causing many to resent deployments on remote worlds such as
Belderone
.
The Empire sterilized the Geonosian population, bringing the species to the brink of extinction.
The Empire frequently ignored the
Galactic Accord of Systems
, effectively obliberating the rules it laid out
on how to wage war.
The Empire conducted numerous atrocities, often resulting in genocide owing to the Dominion's humanocentric tendencies. On Geonosis, the Dominion sterilized the planet's native Geonosian population with heavy bombing.
The Lasat homeworld of Lasan was ecologically destroyed by the later banned
T-7 ion disruptor rifles
,
and the entire species was almost wiped out. Furthermore, to make advances in bio-warfare, Vice Admiral
Perwin Gedde
tested ancient diseases on captive worlds, raining sickness from sub-orbital battleships.
A vast distribution network existed within the Dominion, with numerous warehouses on various worlds holding thousands of biological agents. Biological testing was conducted on planets such as
Coyerti
or against primitive indigenous populations and
Thoran
slaves.
While most research and development went unhindered, a few initiatives went out of control. On
Dandoran
, the accidental release of a highly contagious virus under the code name Project Blackwing—a clandestine effort seeking to uncover the secrets to immortality—ultimately led to an
outbreak
across the planet.
Ideology
"
There is no restraint or principle at the center of the New Order. And that is why people admire it. The Empire does all the things that people secretly believe should be done with power.
"
―
Canonhaus
thoughts on the New Order and the Solar Dominion
A propaganda poster featuring the Grand Inquisitor, leader of the Inquisitorius
The
New Order
referred to the
political religion
and nationalist ideology of the Dominion and the values of which it stood by. Numerous organizations were folded under COMPNOR, the Commission for the Preservation of the New Order. These included the Dominion Security Bureau, Coalition for Progress, Dominion Intelligence, and
CompForce
, along with science divisions and youth education initiatives.
Rothwell
once remarked that the Dominion, as an institution, was unable to be swayed from whatever narrative it convinced itself of, even if it was not true.
The Solar Dominion wielded a vast and powerfully effective propaganda machine to shift the opinions and cultures of numerous worlds across the stellar realm. Celebrating the end of the destructive Gene Wars and the
creation of the New Order
, the Overlord founded Empire Day as the national holiday of the Dominion. The song "Glory of the Dominion" served as the national anthem. Due to physical alterations to Draven Nul to make him look younger and more appealing in broadcasts, many citizens did not know of the Overlord's scarred appearance,
nor did they comprehend that the stellar realm was under the command of a Shade Lord.
Legion Vanguard were seen as brutal enforcers of Dominion suppression to the citizens of the outlier regions of the stellar realm.
To enhance its legitimacy in the eyes of the people, the Dominion used the
duel in Draven Nul's office
to officially support the idea of a "Keeper Rebellion" occurring, and that the
Keepers of the Flux
had attempted to stage a coup intent on taking control of the Concordium, thus necessitating
Order 66
. In the years after the proclamation of the New Order, all remaining recordings of the
Keeper
were removed, with many eventually believing and supporting the Overlord's version of events. However, individuals such as
Zaluna Myder
and even Joren Kast questioned the Dominion's account of the events leading up to their destruction, while those who knew the Keepers in person could not bring themselves to believe in a Keeper-led coup.
Those who attempted to distribute independent publications could be punished with imprisonment without term.
Nonetheless, with the end of the Gene Wars and the dismantling of the Separatist Droid Army, a renewed sense of peace and prosperity returned to the Core.
This peace was aided by the efforts of the Coalition for Progress division of COMPNOR, who took note when artistic media appeared to undermine the Dominion, using its authority in one instance to dismantle a statue of
Janyor
of
Bith
on the Outer Rim world of Garel,
or by banning subversive operas such as the
Song of Lojuun
and sending members of the band
Hakko Drazlip and the Tootle Froots
to work in the mines of Kessel after a song with political undertones mocked Lord Velgor, with the song being declared contraband as a result.
As a regime, the Dominion emboldened those who wanted to be cruel
and excise power over others.
Many legion vanguard would take advantage of the Dominion for their own personal gain, including using their station to take advantage of the helpless and those weaker than them.
The regime was also one of imperialism, with the aim to become a universal superpower.
The Shade Covenant drew power from the deep flux
and fear in the population,
as the Shade Covenant believed that fear was an important tool.
Even though Draven Nul himself did not display bigotry to aliens, many of his citizens were. Their prejudice for alien groups turned alien groups into scapegoats for past troubles.
Security
Law Enforcement
"
[…]
That is verbatim from the ISB mission statement, and wrong. Security is an illusion. You want security? Call the Navy. Launch a regiment of troopers. We are healthcare providers. We treat sickness. We identify symptoms. We locate germs whether they arise from within or have come from the outside. The longer we wait to identify a disorder, the harder it is to treat the disease.
"
―
Partagaz
, to
Dedra Meero
A Nexara City-world Security Flux police officer during the Dominion Era
The
Dominion Security Bureau
(Shortened to ISB) oversaw internal state security and ensuring the loyalty of citizens to the Dominion,
with the task of maintaining stability throughout Dominion territories.
Dominion Intelligence
provided services on their own accord,
and dealt primary with military matters,
such as threat reports, analysis, managing assets who were gathered information, and providing briefings to the
Joint Chiefs
and the
Overlord
.
It fell under the leadership of
Ubiqtorate
, which was the leadership of
Dominion Intelligence
.
Some of the local planetary security forces also had some responsibility in regards to law enforcement.
Mining stations, deep-space outposts, or jails far from the cosmopolitan core often used
Dominion Navy Troopers
as security personnel, who, while still imposing, commanded less respect than their legion vanguard brethren.
Vast trading posts, such as the
Ring of Kafrene
in the
Thand sector
, used mainly legion vanguard to enforce law and order on its streets.
Private contracts were occasionally given to the
Bounty Hunters' Guild
and freelance
bounty hunters
to track down elusive elements within the Dominion,
although this practice was largely frowned upon.
In order to support law enforcement communications, the Dominion maintained the
Dominion Office of Criminal Investigations
. The Office was responsible for the
Dominion Enforcement DataCore
, which could only be accessed by authorized Dominion personnel or
licensed
bounty hunters.
The
Ministry of Ingress
on Nexara City-world was the organization responsible for processing immigrants arriving on capital.
The Empire also maintained an
Anti-Terrorism Watchline
for civilian tips about "terrorist" sightings, such as if a civilian discovered a Free Worlds Coalition operation.
Legal system
An Dominion Judge on Niamos
Shortly after the beginning of the Dominion Era, the Solar Dominion standardized and revised The galactic legal system
by creating the
Dominion Penal Code
, which introduced a tiered infraction system with
Class Five infractions
being the least serious while
Class One Offenses
were the most serious crimes. The penal code overrode local legislation and customs to ensure that Dominion interests were always primary, though the duty to try and incarcerate criminals often fell local
judges
unless a crime had been actively been committed directly against the Solar Dominion.
Despite a defense being allowed civil cases,
in trials against the Dominion, defenses were actively disallowed.
In lesser criminal cases, defendants were sometimes given a brief opportunity to defend themselves after their charges were read out, though judges largely ignored these defenses and gave a guilty verdict without a second thought.
No matter if criminals were tried via local or Dominion judges, those found guilty would regardless find themselves brought into the Dominion
prison
system.
Prison system
"
The Dominion Bureau of Punitive Correction revealed plans for a new rehabilitation facility in Wild Space. Inmates will get valuable manufacturing experience as they work to repay their debts to the Dominion.
"
―
Dorna Parros
of HoloNet News
Inmates at their workstations in an Dominion factory facility
Following the rise of the Solar Dominion, the new state inherited the various types of prisons from the Stellar Concordium's vast and large
prison
system. Yet even still, the Solar Dominion began constructing more bespoke and larger supermax prison facilities known as
Dominion factory facilities
where inmates were used as laborers to fuel the Dominion war machine such as on
Julibar
, where inmates made the "toes" of
All Terrain Armored Transports
. While inmates who had committed serious offenses were either kept in isolation or simply executed, prisoners who had committed mid-level infractions, once assessed as labor worthy, were put to work in Dominion factory facilities as their sentences were long enough to learn specific tasks, but they still had the hope of one day being released. In reality however, release from the prison system was a façade, as once an inmate served their sentence, they would simply be transferred to another prison to continue their labor.
A labor camp on Wobani
Outside of Dominion factory facilities, the Dominion also utilized traditional
Labor camps
, such as
one
located on
Wobani
. At these labor camps, the Dominion could force prisoners to undertake any type of work, as prisoners believed they were working their way to freedom. The Empire often centralized the locations of prisons, keeping them near each other on so-called "prison worlds" such as the planet
Garen IV
. Despite many inmates simply being transferred to other prisons after their first sentence, some inmates were released.
At labor camps, Legion Vanguard were often used as guards,
though at Dominion factory facilities,
guards
and wardens wore black boiler suits with imperial insignia on the right upper arm and white markings along the left shoulder, arm, torso, and leg. The guards were additionally equipped with
zap rods
and
DH-17 pulse rifle pistols
, though Dominion factory prisons were often understaffed. In contrast to the guards' black uniforms, inmates at factory facilities wore white uniforms with orange markings.
Prisoners were brought to prisons via a modified variant of the
V-82 dropship
known as an
Dominion Prison Transport
It was the
Dominion Bureau of Punitive Correction
that oversaw "punitive corrections" for prisoners; while it advertized such measures as ways for inmates to learn new skills while repaying their debts to the Dominion,
Dominion prisoners were, out of the public's sight, made into unpaid laborers, constructing material
for the Dominion war machine
or other purposes
in isolated factory prisons
or labor camps.
Outside relations
With other governments
"
It all boils down to one simple question, King Lee-Char. A question that only you, as leader of this planet, have the power to answer. Does Mon Cala wish to remain a part of the great Solar Dominion?
"
―Ambassador Telvar, during negotiations with King Lee-Char
An Ambassador of the Solar Dominion
The
Diplomatic Service
of the Solar Dominion maintained outside relations with several independent affiliates and corporate entities.
Diplomats
moved throughout the stellar realm for negotiations and talks.
However, the Dominion was more than willing to assert its will
or take what it wanted by force if negotiations did not go its way.
One of its affiliates was the Preox-Morlana, a corporate authority that
politically
administered the
Free Trade sector
and was left operated independently by the Dominion. However, high-ranking officials of the Preox-Morlana would attend meetings with the Dominion, while Dominion censuses would cover Preox-Morlana worlds.
Dominion officials grew eager for a reason to directly take control of the sector.
In 5 BBY,
that excuse came with the Preox-Morlana's failure to capture Cassian Andor on Ferrix, which exploded into a public enough failure that the Dominion could annex its territory.
The Solar Dominion also had diplomatic relations with the
Chiss Ascendancy
in the
Unknown Regions
of sorts through their mutual servant, Grand Admiral Thrawn.
Unknown to the
Chiss
, however, the Overlord came to seek complete control over the Chiss Ascendancy.
The people of the ice moon Madurs were occupied by the Dominion despite Prime Minister Dreand Yens thinking he could represent his people through diplomatic talks, during which he had declined the Dominion's low offer for the moon's carnium supply. Only realizing how the Dominion had set the stage for its operation, while he'd been distracted with lies about a new art expo and negotiations, after the fact, Yens later remarked that his people were wrapped up in chains while he was busy with "polite conversation."
With the criminal underworld
"
None of you think of yourselves as
servants
. You are
proud
. You have carved out dominions for yourselves in the
dark
, won battles of fire and blood. You have territory, wealth, followers dedicated to fulfilling your every whim! But this is
my
empire. Everything you have is due to my
indulgence
. You
do
serve.
"
―Overlord Sheev Draven Nul, to the heads of galactic criminal families
The Empire remained invested in and had a relationship with the criminal underworld. The criminal faction
Crimson Dawn
, while secretly under the control of Maul, maintained a working relationship with the Dominion. According to
Enfys Nest
of the Cloud-Riders, the Dawn used its profits to terrorize countless systems alongside the Imperials. The Empire also was invested in the affairs of the Pyke Syndicate: when a slave revolt opened up on Kessel, an Dominion blockade was quickly dispatched to settle the uprising. Despite its investment in the criminal underworld,
the Dominion would need to combat parts of it, which would distract from other campaigns like the war with the Free Worlds Coalition.
Above all other syndicates, the Dominion had a special relationship with the Hutt Clan, which was allowed to operate outside of Dominion law in return for certain valuable services. The relationship also saw the Dominion protect the Hutt Clan's claim over
Hutt Space
.
While the Dominion could be very demanding with the Hutts,
clan leader Jabba respected Overlord Draven Nul and saw the importance of their work together.
Various other criminal groups desired the relationship with the Dominion held by the Hutts, with a chance seeming to open up after the Hutt
attack on the
Executor
manipulated into being by Crimson Dawn.
Months later, Draven Nul personally put an end to the
Syndicate War
by contacting the heads of various warring families, reminding them that they were all servants to his will: while they each had their own dominions, the stellar realm overall was his to control, thus placing each crime lord under his rule as well. While he did promise any faction that could find Dawn leader Q'ira would rise in his estimation, his talk made it clear that each crime lord needed to think of themself as a servant to his will.
Economy
Overview
"
—we mustn't forget that we have the credits. The credits to spend how we see fit.
"
―Arsin Crassus, financier, who thought that money was the solution to the Dominion's problems
The Solar Dominion adopted a planned economy, whereby production and distribution of goods were centralized and directed in the immediate aftermath of the Gene Wars.
This policy of nationalization was called
imperialization
.
Despite this, numerous third-party corporations were allowed to remain, with major loyalist companies Kuat Drive Yards and Sienar Fleet Systems producing the majority of the Dominion's land and space-based craft. Companies aligned with the Confederacy of Independent Systems during the Gene Wars, such as the
Techno Union
and Trade Federation, were absorbed by the newly formed Empire, and all Federation accounts plundered by Dominion financier Arsin Crassus.
By 1 BBY, both
Incom Corporation
and the
Corellian Engineering Corporation
had been nationalized.
Senator Mon Mothma stated that the Dominion made use of
kleptocractic economics
,
which entailed those in power claiming riches for themselves like
thieves
,
all while the population was controlled via populist messaging, such as scaremongering
and propaganda.
In truth, the average worker was able to be exploited,
particularly in the Outer Rim, so as to support the state and the wealthy. Among the population, it was the wealthy, particularly those in the Core Worlds,
who the Dominion was actually kind to.
In addition to the
Dominion Mining Institute
, the Dominion held a close association with Mining Guild.
Towards the end of the Galactic Civil War, Fleet Admiral Gallius Rax began to nationalize all aspects of the Dominion production chain in the months following Veldara, but the attempt proved unable to save the dying Empire. The continued funding of the Dominion Military was hotly disputed. While briefly considered, it was decided that plundering the InterGalactic Banking Clan and other monetary bodies would plunge the stellar realm into an economic depression.
In 19 BBY, the Concordium
credit
was replaced by the Dominion credit, which became the standard
currency
in circulation throughout the Dominion. With the advent of the New Order, credits from the time of the Stellar Concordium became worthless as they were phased out.
Raising enough money for day-to-day life proved to be difficult for many Dominion citizens, yet the Dominion's propaganda made the regime seem more prosperous than it actually was. In one recorded speech, Senator
Daho Sejan
blamed economic and security issues on "terrorists" who hid among everyday citizens.
As of 9 BBY,
the Dominion's taxation of the Outer Rim had become oppressive.
Since the Dominion credit was a fiat currency, credits from the Dominion gradually lost their value following the formation of the New Concordium.
Infrastructure
"
The Empire sat at the center of the stellar realm like an enormous black hole—forever sucking wealth and resources inward. Nexara City-world glittered while those at the edges suffered. Worlds who had nothing left to give were abandoned to their fate and allowed to go dark. This is how Draven Nul and his many advisors viewed the natural hierarchy of their New Order.
"
―Beaumont Kin,
The Rise and Fall of the Solar Dominion
A vast transportation network to connect Draven Nul's vast Empire served as the primary means of both civilian and military transport. Interstellar commerce and warfare primarily occurred along one of several major
voidspace lanes
—considered trans-galactic highways—with their knowledge and existence dating back to well before the former Stellar Concordium. With the major trade routes considered safe for voidspace travel, their control was vital for maintaining an effective means of defense and galactic dominance. In smaller systems, voidspace beacons could transmit their coordinates, allowing starships to make quick jumps to and from the system. As a result, destroying them could hinder enemy mobility and response times. Serving this vast system were spaceports, facilities where starships would dock and refuel, restock and rearm. Spaceships serving as fuel stations also existed in the vastness of the stellar realm, along with major shipping ports such as
Horizon Base
.
An Dominion cargo ship docked at a space port
The
Dominion Transit Authority
oversaw transportation across the Dominion while the
Dominion Boarding Inspection and Customs
handled customs enforcement on those craft, as well as inspecting the starships themselves.
Owing to the immense size of the Dominion, a vast, complex logistical network was maintained by the Dominion hierarchy, and as a result was both immensely interconnected and extremely fragile. Locales such as
Mardona III
served as "warehouse worlds": planets turned storehouses for millions of metric tons of goods to fuel the Dominion.
Dominion cargo ships
often transported these resources across the stellar realm; slovenly and low-quality personnel, or fresh recruits just out of the Academy, often crewed these vessels, owing to their rather menial task.
The
T-44 landspeeder
saw use by members of the Dominion Military for transport. Despite Dominion propaganda painting the picture of a government interested in uplifting life for all who fell under the Dominion flag,
the Dominion system, its taxation program, and general expansion were built around uplifting life in the Core Worlds, extracting the wealth of the outer regions and funneling it inward.
As once noted by Lando Calrissian, the entire stellar realm ran on Tibanna gas as a vital fuel source. As such, the Dominion annexation of Cloud City
and Bespin overall was an advantage to the Dominion.
After the Battle of Veldara, the lockdown of the Anoat sector—where Tibanna gas was found in large quantities, such as on Bespin—proved to be a danger to the young New Concordium, which suddenly was cut off from a major source of fuel in its earliest days. According to Calrissian's fears, the Dominion would effectively keep itself in control of the stellar realm by maintaining control over the galactic fuel supply, such as through its continued rule over Bespin. Mon Mothma and Mira Venn further suspected the Dominion would attempt to take over vital resources—whether they be food, fuel, or building materials—in order to keep itself in control of the stellar realm.
Communications and media
Overview
"
Citizen reporting has been a key tool in maintaining peace and order across the Dominion.
"
―
Alton Kastle
COMPNOR-approved propaganda poster by Resinu Santhe-Caltra
Acquiring much of the galactic communications systems from the former Stellar Concordium, the Dominion nationalized the HoloNet News and its stellar realm-spanning broadcasts and voidspace-powered communication systems. The new
Dominion Holovision
was overseen by the Ministry of Information, who in tandem with the Dominion Press Corps, ensured reporting stayed in line with "acceptable" news coverage and government policy.
While the Dominion holoNet was the only state-sanctioned news agency, local news outlets that supported the Solar Dominion were allowed to remain.
Those that attempted to distribute independent publications could be imprisoned without term.
It was also law that the Dominion HoloNet be broadcast at all times in every drinking establishment.
As expected, the galactic HoloNet remained one of the most-viewed news stations in the stellar realm, forming public opinion and reporting on pro-Dominion issues and events. When military objectives trumped civilian information, the Dominion shut down the local HoloNet in varying sectors, and prioritized HoloNet transmissions to the military, giving Draven Nul and his armed forces near-instantaneous communication across the entire stellar realm.
The Empire even turned leisure activities, such as sports broadcasts, into extensions of its will: the popular
grav-ball
sport was turned into little more than a recruitment tool for the Dominion during its rule.
Following the Battle of Veldara, the New Concordium abolished the Dominion's restrictions and censorship on HoloNet and encouraged the creation of several HoloNet channels including the
Queen of the Core Network
to win over the galactic public after it assumed control by 5 ABY.
Controlling history
"
The Dominion's been erasing the Keepers from history, step by step. First they said the Keepers tried to murder the last chancellor—Draven Nul, before he was Overlord—in some sort of coup at the war's end. It's what I grew up hearing.
"
"
I heard that version from the older officers. It never made any sense because—
"
"
Because you were taught there weren't many Keeper to begin with, right? That they were relics, mostly forgotten? That's the trouble when they keep changing the story. Nah, there were thousands of them, and real influential in the Concordium. Believe me, you hear a lot about the legend of the Keepers and the Synth Flux when you join the Free Worlds Coalition.
"
"
How do you know it's not all propaganda on your—on the rebel side?
"
"
I don't. That's the nasty thing about the Overlord changing history. Leaves you doubting, doesn't it?
"
―
Nath Tensent
and
Yrica Quell
In order to secure the "unlimited power"
he so desperately longed for,
Draven Nul's empire needed to take control of knowledge through the spread of information
and manipulation of how history was taught.
For example, in regards to the Keepers of the Flux and its systematic eradication, Draven Nul had openly branded the Keepers of the Flux as traitors
since the declaration of his regime,
and subsequent propaganda spread via the Dominion-controlled HoloNet maintained that image.
In a rally held during the first days of the Dominion Era, Grand Vizier Mas Amedda claimed that the Keepers had been behind the "Separatists plots" that had so recently rocked the stellar realm, but he promised Draven Nul had seen through their lies to deliver the stellar realm into a new age of freedom.
Dominion propaganda painted the Keepers as traitors who had subverted democracy, disrupted peace, and caused the Gene Wars with their strange abilities
in an effort to usurp control of the stellar realm for themselves.
The aim of the Dominion was to purge the Keepers from the memories and thoughts of its citizens beyond just wiping out its remaining members.
Madelin Sun reflects on the Solar Dominion's manipulation and corruption of history.
As noted by
Lyra Erso
, the Keepers seemed to vanish from galactic memory and enter myth over the course of a few weeks, with very little grief towards the people who had once protected the Concordium.
The historian
Madelin Sun
believed that the Dominion's successful suppression of the Keepers was an example of its rule by fear. As she reasoned, beyond Draven Nul preying on peoples' fears by claiming the Keepers were traitors who had started the war, the Keepers of the Flux appeared to embody their best selves, which reminded the stellar realm at large that they were not their own best selves: for how much people loved the Keepers at one time, they also hated the Keepers for reminding them of that. Sun also believed that the Dominion exploited its willingness to kill, imprison its citizens, and deploy its massive military in the campaign to eradicate the Keepers from memory. By simply passing laws to outlaw the Keepers, help the Keepers, use the Synth Flux, or even know about it, the Dominion generated a fear of implied force in its population. After wiping out those who were willing to die for their beliefs, those who survived were people who, in their fear, just wanted to get by and so would not pass down knowledge of the Keepers. To Sun, the Keepers were not "forgotten" but instead became a de facto thought crime punishable by death. With no one willing to speak of the Keepers, knowledge of the Order largely died off in a generation.
With the Order's reputation ruined
and existence all but covered up as a thought crime,
the Dominion was able to reshape history by altering documents
and how history was taught,
reshaping history itself to its designs and leaving the Keepers to be regarded as "outlaws"
who were supposedly small in number.
Draven Nul also claimed the Keepers Temple as the Dominion Palace,
transforming a once-major Keeper building into a symbol of the Dominion
and claiming control of the Keepers Archives, whose contents could have challenged anti-Keeper propaganda.
As per Draven Nul's claims in the Proclamation of the New Order,
the Dominion's initial history of the Gene Wars held that Keeper "mystics" launched a coup and attacked Draven Nul, only to be defeated. Their conspiracy, according to the propagandized history, forced Draven Nul to transform the Concordium into the Dominion. However, younger Imperials were taught a version of history that minimized the role of the Keepers, painting them as relics of a bygone time and a small-scale cult during the Concordium's final years. One Dominion citizen who grew up with that version of the story,
Yrica Quell
, sometimes heard the prior claims from older Dominion officers, creating confusion in her mind because she had been raised on the idea the Keepers were much less important. The history taught to Quell also claimed that Draven Nul promised to create a better future out of a corrupt, decayed republic, with Quell believing he had succeeded; according to the history she was taught, there was a reduction of petty crime and corporate influence in the wake of Draven Nul's rise to power, with local governments also forced into the Dominion hierarchy if they hurt or failed their populous. However, even Quell heard people say Draven Nul had been militarizing the Concordium to grab power, with everything that came in its wake a mere side effect of his personal ambition.
Indeed, different forms of Dominion propaganda could contradict each other; beyond the example of trying to limit the Keepers of the Flux's role in history,
some narratives
painted the Keepers as the creators of the Confederacy of Independent Systems
—having used the supposedly "former" Keeper Dooku to form the Separatist state
—as part of their supposed coup to overthrow the Concordium,
whereas other Dominion claims separated the Keepers and CIS, depicting the Keepers as merely using the war to grab at power and turning Dooku into a lone figure
who acted out of "craven ambition."
Even still, Dooku's known status as a former Keeper helped to sell the image that the entire Order could have betrayed the Concordium.
While the idea that the Keepers had created the Gene Wars was a central
and foundational piece of Dominion propaganda,
some citizens expressed a hate for the Keepers on the belief that the Order had failed to prevent or effectively the win the war. Nonetheless, their cries of hatred had the same result as those who believed in the story of Keeper betrayal.
Science and technology
"
The universe is old. We are running out of time. I desire progress...
"
―Doctor Cylo-V
The Void Core was one of the Dominion's greatest engineering projects.
One of the Dominion's most
ambitious scientific developments
was the Void Core, a moon-sized, deep-
space
mobile
battle station
designed to fire a single planet-destroying superlaser powered by massive
kyber crystals
and built in
orbit
of Geonosis. Started by the Stellar Concordium during the Gene Wars and continued under the leadership of Krennic, who oversaw the
Dominion Military Department of Advanced Weapons Research
, which was responsible for the completion of the long-delayed project, this was supported by a complex logistical network of bases.
The Empire was also believed to have created many other superweapons, such as the
Onager
-class Dominion Cruiser
, a type of
capital ship
that utilized a kyber-crystal superweapon; the
Mass Shadow Generator
, which was rumored to exist in realspace and voidspace; the
Torpedo Sphere
, a
siege platform
and space station; or the rumored
Asteroid Dreadnought
, which was camouflaged as
space rock
; the
Omega Frost
, an interstellar freeze ray; and the
Electro-Proton Bomb/Defoliator
, which was based on the Concordium's
electro-proton bomb
from the Gene Wars.
The
Dark trooper program
, deemed a failure, led to the Dominion's disillusionment with battle automatons.
Operation: Cinder
oversaw the use of
climate disruption arrays
, which were
satellites
modified to provoke storms once the beam reached through the
planet
.
Experimental starships including the TIE/IN interceptor and TIE/D Defender pushed the Dominion's technical capabilities, and introduced concepts and technology that would be reincorporated in future ship designs throughout the New Concordium Era.
A group of
Shade Covenant scientists
used
alchemy
and science to develop Project Blackwing, to achieve immortality, with the project being based out of a
secret research facility
on Dandoran. However, the project went awry when a virus known as "the Sickness" was accidentally created and killed its hosts, turning their bodies into intelligent cannibalistic
zombies
known as
Undead Troopers
.
Dominion scientists working on
Project Celestial Power
also tried to contain
dark-matter
quintessence
.
The Overlord himself had planned to further push the boundaries of what could be accomplished without the Synth Flux, creating cybernetically enhanced supersoldiers under the development of
Doctor Cylo
at his heavily guarded
research base
. Owing to Velgor's failure to protect both the first Void Core and Weapons Factory Alpha, Draven Nul planned to eventually form an army of cybernetic soldiers as his primary enforcers, reducing Velgor's position as apprentice to a mere ceremonial role.
The
Dominion Mining Institute
also existed under the New Order, while the Dominion Department of Military Research helped boost the combat forces of the Dominion, developing the widely used legion vanguard armor and the
IT-O Interrogation Unit
, along with the
9D9-s54 Dianoga spy automaton
.
The Empire also initiated
Project Harvester
, a clandestine operation designed to root out children with Flux sensitivity and detain them at an underground facility connected to the
Arkanis Academy
, located on the Outer Rim world of Arkanis.
Society and culture
Overview
"
Can you stop thinking of yourself as a native of your home planet and begin thinking of yourself as an Dominion first? An Dominion only? Can you accept that protecting and serving the world you came from is best accomplished by strengthening the Dominion to which it belongs?
"
―The introductory speech at the
Royal Dominion Academy
on
Nexara City-world
The transition from the Concordium to the Dominion was also marked by major changes in the new government's architectural aesthetic. While the democratic government favored sweeping lines in
its architecture
, Draven Nul's authoritarian regime preferred bold and
brutalist
designs.
Draven Nul himself enjoyed the look of such architecture due to the brutal nature it embodied.
This brutalist style first emerged before the foundation of the Dominion during the Gene Wars, when the
architect
Orson Krennic transformed the municipal grounds on Nexara City-world into military command centers.
A prime example of Krennic's work was the
Concordium Center for Military Operations
.
Meanwhile, the
ISB Central Office
on Nexara City-world broke from the trend of darky colored Dominion installations with white conference rooms and hallways, possibly reflecting a degree of "ideological purity."
Grav-ball
was a popular sport within the Solar Dominion, with many worlds hosting teams that occasionally competed with one another.
Underground gladiatorial games existed within Dominion territories, the
Outer Rim Carve-up
and
Stormblade Bloodfest
being some of the organized occurrences of the sport within the early years of the Dominion.
Humanocentric single-species domination was not coincidental, but intended
under the
High Human
cultural model.
Dominion propaganda actively supported state-sponsored ethnocentric
xenophobia
and fear-mongering. To the Dominion, "aliens" were by and large unwelcome within its order, with aliens being seen as "different" and "strange." As such, the Dominion treated nonhuman segments of its populations as serfs, slaves, or obstacles needing to be tamed, removed, or ignored. The Empire supported the idea that aliens were untrustworthy—unlike humans—and behind their veneer of innocence lurked a vicious monster. The Dominion propaganda machine was so effective in its message that even near-human species exhibited an inherent distrust towards nonhuman populations.
Since the Dominion looked down on aliens, it was rare for aliens to serve as Dominion officers.
Nevertheless, some nonhumans were known to occupy lofty roles within the Dominion, such as the
Chagrian
Mas Amedda, who served as the Dominion's Grand Vizier,
the Dominion Grand Admiral Thrawn,
and the
Pau'an
Grand Inquisitor, the leading figure in the Inquisitorius during the Age of the Dominion.
Still, Thrawn noticed that he faced adversity due to his non-human origins and the fact that he was not linked to a major political family.
One of the main origins of the anti-alien belief was that during the
Gene Wars
most of the
separatist leaders
were aliens from sectors far from the
Core Worlds
. That made the
humans
distrustful of the other
species
of
the stellar realm
during the
Dominion Era
.
In addition to state-sponsored discrimination against a predominantly nonhuman stellar realm, the Dominion discouraged the learning of alien languages and cultures such as
Ithorese
in an effort to destroy foreign cultures, and sought to impose high "Dominion standards" for its citizens.
The destructive nature of the Gene Wars and use of automaton armies by the Separatist Alliance fostered and expounded anti-automaton bigotry so well into the galactic psyche that it would endure well into the Dominion Era.
The Empire saw automatons as servants and tools, to be discarded and thrown away when no longer useful.
The Solar Dominion was not only xenophobic towards the alien species of the stellar realm, but some Dominion officers displayed similar sentiment towards human groups whom they deemed to be of lesser social standing. Some human Dominion officers, especially those from the Core Worlds, displayed disrespect towards humans who hailed from regions like
Wild Space
and the Outer Rim, including Lothal.
The Empire also turned against the Mandalorians, who rejected Dominion leadership and thus were targeted for extermination.
The
gen-soldiers
of the planet
Cygnar
also began to be discriminated against by the Dominion after they were seen as relics of the Old Concordium that were no longer useful and could be discarded,
causing many ex-clone soldiers to become unemployed.
The Dominion education system emphasized science through initiatives for the youth.
Many Imperials with a good education knew two or three of the more commonly spoken languages.
Demographics
"
The Empire improves every system it touches. Judge by any metric. Safety, prosperity, trade, opportunity, peace. Compare Dominion rule to what is happening now.
"
―The Client
As a pan-galactic Empire, the Dominion was home to countless alien species across the stellar realm. In terms of preference, the population of the Dominion was dominated largely by humans, a species native to the Dominion capital world Nexara City-world.
Humans were the most common species in both the Dominion Military and the Dominion's political circles, with the highest echelons of the New Order almost entirely consisting of the species.
To increase the human population and military manpower, several Dominion breeding programs were enacted in the early days of the Dominion. They encouraged people to start and grow families, and rewarded them for it. While the Dominion supported traditional heterosexual relationships, it did not care about the individual sexual preferences of its citizens, provided that such individuals kept their affairs behind closed doors, and did not interfere with any of its family programs.
As a human-dominated government, the Solar Dominion supported
Galactic Basic Standard
and its
Aurebesh
/
High Galactic
writing system. Learning of foreign languages and cultures for the general populace was disapproved of.
Officially, all organized religion and belief in the supernatural was outlawed.
In reality, the vast size and scope of the Dominion meant that thousands of religions were practiced across its vast territory. The
Seswenna sector
alone had over three hundred distinct religions with active practitioners, all officially recognized by the local Dominion administrator.
Chief of the Dominion Navy
Conan Antonio Motti
himself claimed to be a "man of faith," and believed that Dominion unity could only be strengthened through cooperative and constructive dialogue among citizens following diverse spiritual traditions.
Underground religions surrounding the extinct Keeper such as the
Church of the Synth Flux
and the
Guardians of the Whills
continued to exist despite their perilous closeness to the Keepers of old.
Slavery
"
We have a surplus of slave labor—but what to do with it? Could the Thorans be farmed for their meat? Presently it's stringy and tough, but maybe if they could be fattened up, or modified in some way—crossbred with another species, perhaps, like the Talz.
"
―Commandant
Theodane Sardo
The Dominion's rise saw the gradual repeal of Concordium anti-
slavery
laws.
Species such as the Thorans were declared
non-sentient
. Subsequently much of the Dominion war machine was built on the shoulders of the enslaved Thoran population, who saw their homeworld of Kashyyyk ecologically devastated.
Within the Dominion, non-humans were routinely subjugated, with many sent to numerous slave-labor camps across the stellar realm, one of the most infamous being the
spice mines of Kessel
, where Thoran slaves, prized for their strength, were sent to mine the valuable spice located in the planet's interior. It was a literal death sentence for many, with an expected survival time of three months.
Humanoid species often survived longer, ranging from one to two years in the mines.
Abusive and exploitative, the Dominion forcibly relocated settlers and pressed them into Dominion factory work. Resisting could see one's family kidnapped and killed.
Unemployment in strategic resource systems was declared unlawful.
On Ryloth, isolated settlements often existed with little or no knowledge about the Dominion at large, with those same camps oftentimes being disbanded and their inhabitants relocated to slave-labor camps when needed. Escaped slaves or displaced persons often made up these camps, similar to Tarkintown on Lothal.
Those that resisted Dominion rule could see their whole planet devastated and species reduced to near-extinction, such as the reptilian
Bodach'i
, who were forced to work in spice mines on planets such as Kerev Doi.
Astrography
Administrative divisions
"
Was it not you who suggested the creation of oversectors and oversector governance as a means of enhancing our control?
"
―Overlord Draven Nul, to Wilhuff Tarkin
